    25	// The following constant values are derived from the default values in Linux.
    26	
    27	/// Maximum number of semaphore sets.
    28	pub const SEMMNI: usize = 32000;
    29	/// Maximum number of semaphores per semaphore ID.
    30	pub const SEMMSL: usize = 32000;
    31	/// Maximum number of seaphores in all semaphore sets.
    32	pub const SEMMNS: usize = SEMMNI * SEMMSL;
    33	/// Maximum number of operations for semop.
    34	pub const SEMOPM: usize = 500;
    35	/// MAximum semaphore value.
    36	pub const SEMVMX: i32 = 32767;
    37	/// Maximum value that can be recorded for semaphore adjustment (SEM_UNDO).
    38	pub const SEMAEM: i32 = SEMVMX;
    39	
    40	#[derive(Debug)]
    41	pub struct SemaphoreSet {
    42	    /// Number of semaphores in the set
    43	    nsems: usize,
    44	    /// Inner
    45	    inner: SpinLock<SemSetInner>,
    46	    /// Semaphore permission
    47	    permission: IpcPermission,
    48	    /// Creation time or last modification via `semctl`
    49	    sem_ctime: AtomicU64,
    50	    /// Last semop time.
    51	    sem_otime: AtomicU64,
    52	}
    53	
    54	#[derive(Debug)]
    55	pub(super) struct SemSetInner {
    56	    /// Semaphores
    57	    pub(super) sems: Box<[Semaphore]>,
    58	    /// Pending alter operations.
    59	    pub(super) pending_alter: LinkedList<PendingOp>,
    60	    /// Pending zeros operations.
    61	    pub(super) pending_const: LinkedList<PendingOp>,
    62	}
    63	
    64	impl SemSetInner {
    65	    pub fn field_mut(
    66	        &mut self,
    67	    ) -> (
    68	        &mut Box<[Semaphore]>,
    69	        &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
    70	        &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
    71	    ) {
    72	        (
    73	            &mut self.sems,
    74	            &mut self.pending_alter,
    75	            &mut self.pending_const,
    76	        )
    77	    }
    78	}
    79	
    80	impl SemaphoreSet {
    81	    pub fn pending_const_count(&self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
    82	        let inner = self.inner.lock();
    83	        let pending_const = &inner.pending_const;
    84	        let mut count = 1;
    85	        for i in pending_const.iter() {
    86	            for sem_buf in i.sops_iter() {
    87	                if sem_buf.sem_num() == sem_num {
    88	                    count += 1;
    89	                }
    90	            }
    91	        }
    92	        count
    93	    }
    94	
    95	    pub fn pending_alter_count(&self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
    96	        let inner = self.inner.lock();
    97	        let pending_alter = &inner.pending_alter;
    98	        let mut count = 1;
    99	        for i in pending_alter.iter() {
   100	            for sem_buf in i.sops_iter() {
   101	                if sem_buf.sem_num() == sem_num {
   102	                    count += 1;
   103	                }
   104	            }
   105	        }
   106	        count
   107	    }
   108	
   109	    pub fn nsems(&self) -> usize {
   110	        self.nsems
   111	    }
   112	
   113	    pub fn setval(&self, sem_num: usize, val: i32, pid: Pid) -> Result<()> {
   114	        if !(0..SEMVMX).contains(&val) {
   115	            return_errno!(Errno::ERANGE);
   116	        }
   117	
   118	        let mut inner = self.inner();
   119	        let (sems, pending_alter, pending_const) = inner.field_mut();
   120	        let sem = sems.get_mut(sem_num).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;
   121	
   122	        sem.set_val(val);
   123	        sem.set_latest_modified_pid(pid);
   124	
   125	        let mut wake_queue = LinkedList::new();
   126	        if val == 0 {
   127	            wake_const_ops(sems, pending_const, &mut wake_queue);
   128	        } else {
   129	            update_pending_alter(sems, pending_alter, pending_const, &mut wake_queue);
   130	        }
   131	
   132	        for wake_op in wake_queue {
   133	            wake_op.set_status(Status::Normal);
   134	            if let Some(waker) = wake_op.waker() {
   135	                waker.wake_up();
   136	            }
   137	        }
   138	
   139	        self.update_ctime();
   140	        Ok(())
   141	    }
   142	
   143	    pub fn get<T>(&self, sem_num: usize, func: &dyn Fn(&Semaphore) -> T) -> Result<T> {
   144	        let inner = self.inner();
   145	        Ok(func(
   146	            inner.sems.get(sem_num).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?,
   147	        ))
   148	    }
   149	
   150	    pub fn permission(&self) -> &IpcPermission {
   151	        &self.permission
   152	    }
   153	
   154	    pub fn sem_ctime(&self) -> Duration {
   155	        Duration::from_secs(self.sem_ctime.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   156	    }
   157	
   158	    pub fn update_ctime(&self) {
   159	        self.sem_ctime.store(
   160	            RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time().as_secs(),
   161	            Ordering::Relaxed,
   162	        );
   163	    }
   164	
   165	    pub fn update_otime(&self) {
   166	        self.sem_otime.store(
   167	            RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time().as_secs(),
   168	            Ordering::Relaxed,
   169	        );
   170	    }
   171	
   172	    pub(super) fn inner(&self) -> SpinLockGuard<SemSetInner, PreemptDisabled> {
   173	        self.inner.lock()
   174	    }
   175	
   176	    fn new(key: key_t, nsems: usize, mode: u16, credentials: Credentials<ReadOp>) -> Result<Self> {
   177	        debug_assert!(nsems <= SEMMSL);
   178	
   179	        let mut sems = Vec::with_capacity(nsems);
   180	        for _ in 0..nsems {
   181	            sems.push(Semaphore::new(0));
   182	        }
   183	
   184	        let permission =
   185	            IpcPermission::new_sem_perm(key, credentials.euid(), credentials.egid(), mode);
   186	
   187	        Ok(Self {
   188	            nsems,
   189	            permission,
   190	            sem_ctime: AtomicU64::new(RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time().as_secs()),
   191	            sem_otime: AtomicU64::new(0),
   192	            inner: SpinLock::new(SemSetInner {
   193	                sems: sems.into_boxed_slice(),
   194	                pending_alter: LinkedList::new(),
   195	                pending_const: LinkedList::new(),
   196	            }),
   197	        })
   198	    }
   199	}
   200	
   201	impl Drop for SemaphoreSet {
   202	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   203	        let mut inner = self.inner();
   204	        let pending_alter = &mut inner.pending_alter;
   205	        for pending_alter in pending_alter.iter_mut() {
   206	            pending_alter.set_status(Status::Removed);
   207	            if let Some(ref waker) = pending_alter.waker() {
   208	                waker.wake_up();
   209	            }
   210	        }
   211	        pending_alter.clear();
   212	
   213	        let pending_const = &mut inner.pending_const;
   214	        for pending_const in pending_const.iter_mut() {
   215	            pending_const.set_status(Status::Removed);
   216	            if let Some(ref waker) = pending_const.waker() {
   217	                waker.wake_up();
   218	            }
   219	        }
   220	        pending_const.clear();
   221	
   222	        ID_ALLOCATOR
   223	            .get()
   224	            .unwrap()
   225	            .lock()
   226	            .free(self.permission.key() as usize);
   227	    }
   228	}

   230	pub fn create_sem_set_with_id(
   231	    id: key_t,
   232	    nsems: usize,
   233	    mode: u16,
   234	    credentials: Credentials<ReadOp>,
   235	) -> Result<()> {
   236	    debug_assert!(nsems <= SEMMSL);
   237	    debug_assert!(id > 0);
   238	
   239	    ID_ALLOCATOR
   240	        .get()
   241	        .unwrap()
   242	        .lock()
   243	        .alloc_specific(id as usize)
   244	        .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EEXIST))?;
   245	
   246	    let mut sem_sets = SEMAPHORE_SETS.write();
   247	    sem_sets.insert(id, SemaphoreSet::new(id, nsems, mode, credentials)?);
   248	
   249	    Ok(())
   250	}
   251	
   252	/// Checks the semaphore. Return Ok if the semaphore exists and pass the check.
   253	pub fn check_sem(id: key_t, nsems: Option<usize>, required_perm: PermissionMode) -> Result<()> {
   254	    debug_assert!(id > 0);
   255	
   256	    let sem_sets = SEMAPHORE_SETS.read();
   257	    let sem_set = sem_sets.get(&id).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::ENOENT))?;
   258	
   259	    if let Some(nsems) = nsems {
   260	        debug_assert!(nsems <= SEMMSL);
   261	        if nsems > sem_set.nsems() {
   262	            return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
   263	        }
   264	    }
   265	
   266	    if !required_perm.is_empty() {
   267	        // TODO: Support permission check
   268	        warn!("Semaphore doesn't support permission check now");
   269	    }
   270	
   271	    Ok(())
   272	}
   273	
   274	pub fn create_sem_set(nsems: usize, mode: u16, credentials: Credentials<ReadOp>) -> Result<key_t> {
   275	    debug_assert!(nsems <= SEMMSL);
   276	
   277	    let id = ID_ALLOCATOR
   278	        .get()
   279	        .unwrap()
   280	        .lock()
   281	        .alloc()
   282	        .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::ENOSPC))? as i32;
   283	
   284	    let mut sem_sets = SEMAPHORE_SETS.write();
   285	    sem_sets.insert(id, SemaphoreSet::new(id, nsems, mode, credentials)?);
   286	
   287	    Ok(id)
   288	}
   289	
   290	pub fn sem_sets<'a>() -> RwLockReadGuard<'a, BTreeMap<key_t, SemaphoreSet>> {
   291	    SEMAPHORE_SETS.read()
   292	}
   293	
   294	pub fn sem_sets_mut<'a>() -> RwLockWriteGuard<'a, BTreeMap<key_t, SemaphoreSet>> {
   295	    SEMAPHORE_SETS.write()
   296	}

   303	pub(super) fn init() {
   304	    ID_ALLOCATOR.call_once(|| {
   305	        let mut id_alloc = IdAlloc::with_capacity(SEMMNI + 1);
   306	        // Remove the first index 0
   307	        id_alloc.alloc();
   308	
   309	        SpinLock::new(id_alloc)
   310	    });
   311	}

   136	pub fn sem_op(
   137	    sem_id: key_t,
   138	    sops: Vec<SemBuf>,
   139	    timeout: Option<Duration>,
   140	    ctx: &Context,
   141	) -> Result<()> {
   142	    debug_assert!(sem_id > 0);
   143	    debug!("[semop] sops: {:?}", sops);
   144	
   145	    let pid = ctx.process.pid();
   146	    let mut pending_op = PendingOp {
   147	        sops,
   148	        status: Arc::new(AtomicStatus::new(Status::Pending)),
   149	        waker: None,
   150	        pid,
   151	    };
   152	
   153	    // TODO: Support permission check
   154	    warn!("Semaphore operation doesn't support permission check now");
   155	
   156	    let (alter, dupsop) = get_sops_flags(&pending_op);
   157	    if dupsop {
   158	        warn!("Found duplicate sop");
   159	    }
   160	
   161	    let local_sem_sets = sem_sets();
   162	    let sem_set = local_sem_sets
   163	        .get(&sem_id)
   164	        .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;
   165	    let mut inner = sem_set.inner();
   166	
   167	    if perform_atomic_semop(&mut inner.sems, &mut pending_op)? {
   168	        if alter {
   169	            let wake_queue = do_smart_update(&mut inner, &pending_op);
   170	            for wake_op in wake_queue {
   171	                wake_op.set_status(Status::Normal);
   172	                if let Some(waker) = wake_op.waker {
   173	                    waker.wake_up();
   174	                }
   175	            }
   176	        }
   177	
   178	        sem_set.update_otime();
   179	        return Ok(());
   180	    }
   181	
   182	    // Prepare to wait
   183	    let status = pending_op.status.clone();
   184	    let (waiter, waker) = Waiter::new_pair();
   185	
   186	    // Check if timeout exists to avoid calling `Arc::clone()`
   187	    if let Some(timeout) = timeout {
   188	        pending_op.waker = Some(waker.clone());
   189	
   190	        let jiffies_timer = JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER.get().unwrap().create_timer(move || {
   191	            waker.wake_up();
   192	        });
   193	        jiffies_timer.set_timeout(Timeout::After(timeout));
   194	    } else {
   195	        pending_op.waker = Some(waker);
   196	    }
   197	
   198	    if alter {
   199	        inner.pending_alter.push_back(pending_op);
   200	    } else {
   201	        inner.pending_const.push_back(pending_op);
   202	    }
   203	
   204	    drop(inner);
   205	    drop(local_sem_sets);
   206	
   207	    waiter.wait();
   208	    match status.status() {
   209	        Status::Normal => Ok(()),
   210	        Status::Removed => Err(Error::new(Errno::EIDRM)),
   211	        Status::Pending => {
   212	            // FIXME: Getting sem_sets maybe time-consuming.
   213	            let sem_sets = sem_sets();
   214	            let sem_set = sem_sets.get(&sem_id).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;
   215	            let mut inner = sem_set.inner();
   216	
   217	            let pending_ops = if alter {
   218	                &mut inner.pending_alter
   219	            } else {
   220	                &mut inner.pending_const
   221	            };
   222	            pending_ops.retain(|op| op.pid != pid);
   223	
   224	            Err(Error::new(Errno::EAGAIN))
   225	        }
   226	    }
   227	}
   228	
   229	/// Update pending const and alter operations, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L1029>
   230	pub(super) fn do_smart_update(
   231	    inner: &mut SpinLockGuard<SemSetInner, PreemptDisabled>,
   232	    pending_op: &PendingOp,
   233	) -> LinkedList<PendingOp> {
   234	    let mut wake_queue = LinkedList::new();
   235	
   236	    let (sems, pending_alter, pending_const) = inner.field_mut();
   237	
   238	    if !pending_const.is_empty() {
   239	        do_smart_wakeup_zero(sems, pending_const, pending_op, &mut wake_queue);
   240	    }
   241	    if !pending_alter.is_empty() {
   242	        update_pending_alter(sems, pending_alter, pending_const, &mut wake_queue);
   243	    }
   244	
   245	    wake_queue
   246	}
   247	
   248	/// Look for pending alter operations that can be completed, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L949>
   249	pub(super) fn update_pending_alter(
   250	    sems: &mut Box<[Semaphore]>,
   251	    pending_alter: &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
   252	    pending_const: &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
   253	    wake_queue: &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
   254	) {
   255	    let mut cursor = pending_alter.cursor_front_mut();
   256	    while let Some(alter_op) = cursor.current() {
   257	        if let Ok(true) = perform_atomic_semop(sems, alter_op) {
   258	            let mut alter_op = cursor.remove_current_as_list().unwrap();
   259	
   260	            do_smart_wakeup_zero(sems, pending_const, alter_op.front().unwrap(), wake_queue);
   261	
   262	            wake_queue.append(&mut alter_op);
   263	        } else {
   264	            cursor.move_next();
   265	        }
   266	    }
   267	}
   268	
   269	/// Wakeup all wait for zero tasks, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L893>
   270	fn do_smart_wakeup_zero(
   271	    sems: &mut Box<[Semaphore]>,
   272	    pending_const: &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
   273	    pending_op: &PendingOp,
   274	    wake_queue: &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
   275	) {
   276	    for sop in pending_op.sops_iter() {
   277	        if sems.get(sop.sem_num as usize).unwrap().val == 0 {
   278	            wake_const_ops(sems, pending_const, wake_queue);
   279	            return;
   280	        }
   281	    }
   282	}
   283	
   284	/// Wakeup pending const operations, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L854>
   285	pub(super) fn wake_const_ops(
   286	    sems: &mut Box<[Semaphore]>,
   287	    pending_const: &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
   288	    wake_queue: &mut LinkedList<PendingOp>,
   289	) {
   290	    let mut cursor = pending_const.cursor_front_mut();
   291	    while let Some(const_op) = cursor.current() {
   292	        if let Ok(true) = perform_atomic_semop(sems, const_op) {
   293	            wake_queue.append(&mut cursor.remove_current_as_list().unwrap());
   294	        } else {
   295	            cursor.move_next();
   296	        }
   297	    }
   298	}
   299	
   300	/// Iter the sops and return the flags (alter, dupsop)
   301	fn get_sops_flags(pending_op: &PendingOp) -> (bool, bool) {
   302	    let mut alter = false;
   303	    let mut dupsop = false;
   304	    let mut dup = 0;
   305	    for sop in pending_op.sops_iter() {
   306	        let mask: u64 = 1 << ((sop.sem_num) % 64);
   307	
   308	        if (dup & mask) != 0 {
   309	            dupsop = true;
   310	        }
   311	
   312	        if sop.sem_op != 0 {
   313	            alter = true;
   314	            dup |= mask;
   315	        }
   316	    }
   317	    (alter, dupsop)
   318	}
   319	
   320	/// Perform atomic semop, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L719>
   321	/// 1. Return Ok(true) if the operation success.
   322	/// 2. Return Ok(false) if the caller needs to wait.
   323	/// 3. Return Err(err) if the operation cause error.
   324	fn perform_atomic_semop(sems: &mut Box<[Semaphore]>, pending_op: &mut PendingOp) -> Result<bool> {
   325	    let mut result;
   326	    for op in pending_op.sops_iter() {
   327	        let sem = sems.get(op.sem_num as usize).ok_or(Errno::EFBIG)?;
   328	        let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(op.sem_flags as u32);
   329	        result = sem.val();
   330	
   331	        // Zero condition
   332	        if op.sem_op == 0 && result != 0 {
   333	            if flags.contains(IpcFlags::IPC_NOWAIT) {
   334	                return_errno!(Errno::EAGAIN);
   335	            } else {
   336	                return Ok(false);
   337	            }
   338	        }
   339	
   340	        result += i32::from(op.sem_op);
   341	        if result < 0 {
   342	            if flags.contains(IpcFlags::IPC_NOWAIT) {
   343	                return_errno!(Errno::EAGAIN);
   344	            } else {
   345	                return Ok(false);
   346	            }
   347	        }
   348	
   349	        if result > SEMVMX {
   350	            return_errno!(Errno::ERANGE);
   351	        }
   352	        if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
   353	            todo!()
   354	        }
   355	    }
   356	
   357	    // Success, do operation
   358	    for op in pending_op.sops_iter() {
   359	        let sem = &mut sems[op.sem_num as usize];
   360	        if op.sem_op != 0 {
   361	            sem.val += i32::from(op.sem_op);
   362	            sem.latest_modified_pid = pending_op.pid;
   363	        }
   364	    }
   365	
   366	    Ok(true)
   367	}

    15	pub fn sys_semop(sem_id: i32, tsops: Vaddr, nsops: usize, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    16	    debug!(
    17	        "[sys_semop] sem_id = {:?}, tsops_vaddr = {:x?}, nsops = {:?}",
    18	        sem_id, tsops, nsops
    19	    );
    20	    do_sys_semtimedop(sem_id, tsops, nsops, None, ctx)
    21	}
    22	
    23	pub fn sys_semtimedop(
    24	    sem_id: i32,
    25	    tsops: Vaddr,
    26	    nsops: usize,
    27	    timeout: Vaddr,
    28	    ctx: &Context,
    29	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    30	    debug!(
    31	        "[sys_semtimedop] sem_id = {:?}, tsops_vaddr = {:x?}, nsops = {:?}, timeout_vaddr = {:x?}",
    32	        sem_id, tsops, nsops, timeout
    33	    );
    34	
    35	    let timeout = if timeout == 0 {
    36	        None
    37	    } else {
    38	        Some(Duration::try_from(
    39	            ctx.get_user_space().read_val::<timespec_t>(timeout)?,
    40	        )?)
    41	    };
    42	
    43	    do_sys_semtimedop(sem_id, tsops, nsops, timeout, ctx)
    44	}
    45	
    46	fn do_sys_semtimedop(
    47	    sem_id: i32,
    48	    tsops: Vaddr,
    49	    nsops: usize,
    50	    timeout: Option<Duration>,
    51	    ctx: &Context,
    52	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    53	    if sem_id <= 0 || nsops == 0 {
    54	        return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
    55	    }
    56	    if nsops > SEMOPM {
    57	        return_errno!(Errno::E2BIG);
    58	    }
    59	
    60	    let user_space = ctx.get_user_space();
    61	    let mut semops = Vec::with_capacity(nsops);
    62	    for i in 0..nsops {
    63	        semops.push(user_space.read_val::<SemBuf>(tsops + size_of::<SemBuf>() * i)?);
    64	    }
    65	
    66	    sem_op(sem_id, semops, timeout, ctx)?;
    67	
    68	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    69	}

    11	        IpcControlCmd,
    12	    },
    13	    prelude::*,
    14	    process::Pid,
    15	};
    16	
    17	pub fn sys_semctl(
    18	    semid: i32,
    19	    semnum: i32,
    20	    cmd: i32,
    21	    arg: Vaddr,
    22	    ctx: &Context,
    23	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    24	    if semid <= 0 || semnum < 0 {
    25	        return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL)
    26	    }
    27	
    28	    let cmd = IpcControlCmd::try_from(cmd)?;
    29	    debug!(
    30	        "[sys_semctl] semid = {}, semnum = {}, cmd = {:?}, arg = {:x}",
    31	        semid, semnum, cmd, arg
    32	    );
    33	
    34	    match cmd {
    35	        IpcControlCmd::IPC_RMID => {
    36	            let mut sem_sets_mut = sem_sets_mut();
    37	            let sem_set = sem_sets_mut.get(&semid).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;
    38	
    39	            let euid = ctx.posix_thread.credentials().euid();
    40	            let permission = sem_set.permission();
    41	            let can_removed = (euid == permission.uid()) || (euid == permission.cuid());
    42	            if !can_removed {
    43	                return_errno!(Errno::EPERM);
    44	            }
    45	
    46	            sem_sets_mut
    47	                .remove(&semid)
    48	                .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;
    49	        }
    50	        IpcControlCmd::SEM_SETVAL => {
    51	            // In setval, arg is parse as i32
    52	            let val = arg as i32;
    53	            if val < 0 {
    54	                return_errno!(Errno::ERANGE);
    55	            }
    56	
    57	            check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::ALTER, |sem_set| {
    58	                sem_set.setval(semnum as usize, val, ctx.process.pid())
    59	            })?;
    60	        }
    61	        IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETVAL => {
    62	            fn sem_val(sem: &Semaphore) -> i32 {
    63	                sem.val()
    64	            }
    65	            let val: i32 = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
    66	                sem_set.get(semnum as usize, &sem_val)
    67	            })?;
    68	
    69	            return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(val as isize));
    70	        }
    71	        IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETPID => {
    72	            fn sem_pid(sem: &Semaphore) -> Pid {
    73	                sem.latest_modified_pid()
    74	            }
    75	            let pid: Pid = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
    76	                sem_set.get(semnum as usize, &sem_pid)
    77	            })?;
    78	
    79	            return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(pid as isize));
    80	        }
    81	        IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETZCNT => {
    82	            let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
    83	                Ok(sem_set.pending_const_count(semnum as u16))
    84	            })?;
    85	
    86	            return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(cnt as isize));
    87	        }
    88	        IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETNCNT => {
    89	            let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
    90	                Ok(sem_set.pending_alter_count(semnum as u16))
    91	            })?;
    92	
    93	            return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(cnt as isize));
    94	        }
    95	        _ => todo!("Need to support {:?} in SYS_SEMCTL", cmd),
    96	    }
    97	
    98	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    99	}
   100	
   101	fn check_and_ctl<T, F>(semid: i32, permission: PermissionMode, ctl_func: F) -> Result<T>
   102	where
   103	    F: FnOnce(&SemaphoreSet) -> Result<T>,
   104	{
   105	    check_sem(semid, None, permission)?;
   106	    let sem_sets = sem_sets();
   107	    let sem_set = sem_sets.get(&semid).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;
   108	    ctl_func.call_once((sem_set,))
   109	}

    43	#[repr(u16)]
    44	#[derive(Debug, TryFromInt, Clone, Copy)]
    45	enum Status {
    46	    Normal = 0,
    47	    Pending = 1,
    48	    Removed = 2,
    49	}
    50	
    51	struct AtomicStatus(AtomicU16);
    52	
    53	impl AtomicStatus {
    54	    fn new(status: Status) -> Self {
    55	        Self(AtomicU16::new(status as u16))
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    fn status(&self) -> Status {
    59	        Status::try_from(self.0.load(Ordering::Relaxed)).unwrap()
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    fn set_status(&self, status: Status) {
    63	        self.0.store(status as u16, Ordering::Relaxed);
    64	    }

    67	struct PendingOp {
    68	    sem_buf: SemBuf,
    69	    status: Arc<AtomicStatus>,
    70	    waker: Arc<Waker>,
    71	    pid: Pid,
    72	    process: Weak<Process>,
    73	}
    74	
    75	impl Debug for PendingOp {
    76	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut core::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> core::fmt::Result {
    77	        f.debug_struct("PendingOp")

    80	            .field("pid", &self.pid)
    81	            .finish()

    85	#[derive(Debug)]
    86	pub struct Semaphore {
    87	    val: SpinLock<i32>,
    88	    /// PID of the process that last modified semaphore.
    89	    /// - through semop with op != 0
    90	    /// - through semctl with SETVAL and SETALL
    91	    /// - through SEM_UNDO when task exit
    92	    latest_modified_pid: AtomicU32,
    93	    /// Pending alter operations. For each pending operation, it has `sem_op < 0`.
    94	    pending_alters: SpinLock<LinkedList<PendingOp>>,
    95	    /// Pending zeros operations. For each pending operation, it has `sem_op = 0`.
    96	    pending_const: SpinLock<LinkedList<PendingOp>>,
    97	    /// Last semop time.
    98	    sem_otime: AtomicU64,
    99	}
   100	
   101	impl Semaphore {
   102	    pub fn set_val(&self, val: i32, current_pid: Pid) -> Result<()> {
   103	        if !(0..SEMVMX).contains(&val) {
   104	            return_errno!(Errno::ERANGE);
   105	        }
   106	
   107	        let mut current_val = self.val.lock();
   108	        *current_val = val;
   109	
   110	        self.update_pending_ops(current_val, current_pid);
   111	        Ok(())
   112	    }
   113	
   114	    pub fn val(&self) -> i32 {
   115	        *self.val.lock()
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    pub fn last_modified_pid(&self) -> Pid {
   119	        self.latest_modified_pid.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   120	    }
   121	
   122	    pub fn sem_otime(&self) -> Duration {
   123	        Duration::from_secs(self.sem_otime.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   124	    }
   125	
   126	    pub fn pending_zero_count(&self) -> usize {
   127	        self.pending_const.lock().len()
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    pub fn pending_alter_count(&self) -> usize {
   131	        self.pending_alters.lock().len()
   132	    }
   133	
   134	    /// Notifies the semaphore that the semaphore sets it belongs to have been removed.
   135	    pub(super) fn removed(&self) {
   136	        let mut pending_alters = self.pending_alters.lock();
   137	        for pending_alter in pending_alters.iter_mut() {
   138	            pending_alter.status.set_status(Status::Removed);
   139	            pending_alter.waker.wake_up();
   140	        }
   141	        pending_alters.clear();
   142	
   143	        let mut pending_const = self.pending_const.lock();
   144	        for pending_const in pending_const.iter_mut() {
   145	            pending_const.status.set_status(Status::Removed);
   146	            pending_const.waker.wake_up();
   147	        }
   148	        pending_const.clear();
   149	    }
   150	
   151	    pub(super) fn new(val: i32) -> Self {
   152	        Self {
   153	            val: SpinLock::new(val),
   154	            latest_modified_pid: AtomicU32::new(current!().pid()),
   155	            pending_alters: SpinLock::new(LinkedList::new()),
   156	            pending_const: SpinLock::new(LinkedList::new()),
   157	            sem_otime: AtomicU64::new(0),
   158	        }
   159	    }
   160	
   161	    fn update_otime(&self) {
   162	        self.sem_otime.store(
   163	            RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time().as_secs(),
   164	            Ordering::Relaxed,
   165	        );
   166	    }
   167	
   168	    fn sem_op(&self, sem_buf: &SemBuf, timeout: Option<Duration>, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
   169	        let mut val = self.val.lock();
   170	        let sem_op = sem_buf.sem_op;
   171	        let current_pid = ctx.process.pid();
   172	
   173	        let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(sem_buf.sem_flags as u32);
   174	        if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
   175	            todo!()
   176	        }
   177	
   178	        // Operate val
   179	        let positive_condition = sem_op.is_positive();
   180	        let negative_condition = sem_op.is_negative() && sem_op.abs() as i32 <= *val;
   181	        let zero_condition = sem_op == 0 && *val == 0;
   182	
   183	        if positive_condition || negative_condition {
   184	            let new_val = val
   185	                .checked_add(i32::from(sem_op))
   186	                .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::ERANGE))?;
   187	            if new_val > SEMVMX {
   188	                return_errno!(Errno::ERANGE);
   189	            }
   190	
   191	            *val = new_val;
   192	            self.update_pending_ops(val, current_pid);
   193	            return Ok(());
   194	        } else if zero_condition {
   195	            return Ok(());
   196	        }
   197	        drop(val);
   198	
   199	        // Need to wait for the semaphore
   200	        if flags.contains(IpcFlags::IPC_NOWAIT) {
   201	            return_errno!(Errno::EAGAIN);
   202	        }
   203	
   204	        // Add current to pending list
   205	        let (waiter, waker) = Waiter::new_pair();
   206	        let status = Arc::new(AtomicStatus::new(Status::Pending));
   207	        let pending_op = PendingOp {
   208	            sem_buf: *sem_buf,
   209	            status: status.clone(),
   210	            waker: waker.clone(),
   211	            process: ctx.posix_thread.weak_process(),
   212	            pid: current_pid,
   213	        };
   214	        if sem_op == 0 {
   215	            self.pending_const.lock().push_back(pending_op);
   216	        } else {
   217	            self.pending_alters.lock().push_back(pending_op);
   218	        }
   219	
   220	        // Wait
   221	        if let Some(timeout) = timeout {
   222	            let jiffies_timer = JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER.get().unwrap().create_timer(move || {
   223	                waker.wake_up();
   224	            });
   225	            jiffies_timer.set_timeout(Timeout::After(timeout));
   226	        }
   227	        waiter.wait();
   228	
   229	        // Check status and return
   230	        match status.status() {
   231	            Status::Normal => Ok(()),
   232	            Status::Removed => Err(Error::new(Errno::EIDRM)),
   233	            Status::Pending => {
   234	                let mut pending_ops = if sem_op == 0 {
   235	                    self.pending_const.lock()
   236	                } else {
   237	                    self.pending_alters.lock()
   238	                };
   239	                pending_ops.retain(|op| op.pid != current_pid);
   240	                Err(Error::new(Errno::EAGAIN))
   241	            }
   242	        }
   243	    }
   244	
   245	    /// Updates pending ops after the val changed.
   246	    fn update_pending_ops(&self, mut val: SpinLockGuard<i32, PreemptDisabled>, current_pid: Pid) {
   247	        debug_assert!(*val >= 0);
   248	        trace!("Updating pending ops, semaphore before: {:?}", *val);
   249	
   250	        // Two steps:
   251	        // 1. Remove the pending_alters with `sem_op < 0` if it can.
   252	        // 2. If val is equal to 0, then clear pending_const
   253	
   254	        // Step one:
   255	        let mut value = *val;
   256	        let mut latest_modified_pid = current_pid;
   257	        if value > 0 {
   258	            let mut pending_alters = self.pending_alters.lock();
   259	            let mut cursor = pending_alters.cursor_front_mut();
   260	            while let Some(op) = cursor.current() {
   261	                if value == 0 {
   262	                    break;
   263	                }
   264	                // Check if the process alive.
   265	                if op.process.upgrade().is_none() {
   266	                    cursor.remove_current().unwrap();
   267	                    continue;
   268	                }
   269	
   270	                debug_assert!(op.sem_buf.sem_op < 0);
   271	
   272	                if op.sem_buf.sem_op.abs() as i32 <= value {
   273	                    trace!(
   274	                        "Found removable pending op, op: {:?}, pid:{:?}",
   275	                        op.sem_buf.sem_op,
   276	                        op.pid
   277	                    );
   278	
   279	                    value += i32::from(op.sem_buf.sem_op);
   280	                    latest_modified_pid = op.pid;
   281	                    op.status.set_status(Status::Normal);
   282	                    op.waker.wake_up();
   283	                    cursor.remove_current().unwrap();
   284	                } else {
   285	                    cursor.move_next();
   286	                }
   287	            }
   288	        }
   289	
   290	        if latest_modified_pid != 0 {
   291	            self.latest_modified_pid
   292	                .store(latest_modified_pid, Ordering::Relaxed);
   293	            self.update_otime();
   294	        }
   295	
   296	        // Step two:
   297	        if value == 0 {
   298	            let mut pending_const = self.pending_const.lock();
   299	            pending_const.iter().for_each(|op| {
   300	                op.status.set_status(Status::Normal);
   301	                if op.process.upgrade().is_some() {
   302	                    trace!("Found removable pending op, op: 0, pid:{:?}", op.pid);
   303	                    op.waker.wake_up();
   304	                }
   305	            });
   306	            pending_const.clear();
   307	        }
   308	        *val = value;
   309	        trace!("Updated pending ops, semaphore after: {:?}", value);
   310	    }
   311	}
   312	
   313	pub fn sem_op(
   314	    sem_id: key_t,
   315	    sem_buf: &SemBuf,
   316	    timeout: Option<Duration>,
   317	    ctx: &Context,
   318	) -> Result<()> {
   319	    debug_assert!(sem_id > 0);
   320	    debug!("[semop] sembuf: {:?}", sem_buf);
   321	
   322	    let sem = {
   323	        let sem_sets = sem_sets();
   324	        let sem_set = sem_sets.get(&sem_id).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;
   325	        // TODO: Support permission check
   326	        warn!("Semaphore operation doesn't support permission check now");
   327	
   328	        sem_set
   329	            .get(sem_buf.sem_num as usize)
   330	            .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EFBIG))?
   331	            .clone()
   332	    };
   333	
   334	    sem.sem_op(sem_buf, timeout, ctx)
   335	}

   180	impl Default for WaitQueue {
   181	    fn default() -> Self {
   182	        Self::new()

   185	
   186	/// A waiter that can put the current thread to sleep until it is woken up by the associated
   187	/// [`Waker`].
   188	///
   189	/// By definition, a waiter belongs to the current thread, so it cannot be sent to another thread
   190	/// and its reference cannot be shared between threads.
   191	pub struct Waiter {
   192	    waker: Arc<Waker>,
   193	}
   194	
   195	impl !Send for Waiter {}
   196	impl !Sync for Waiter {}
   197	
   198	/// A waker that can wake up the associated [`Waiter`].
   199	///
   200	/// A waker can be created by calling [`Waiter::new_pair`]. This method creates an `Arc<Waker>` that can
   201	/// be used across different threads.
   202	pub struct Waker {
   203	    has_woken: AtomicBool,
   204	    task: Arc<Task>,
   205	}
   206	
   207	impl Waiter {
   208	    /// Creates a waiter and its associated [`Waker`].
   209	    pub fn new_pair() -> (Self, Arc<Waker>) {
   210	        let waker = Arc::new(Waker {
   211	            has_woken: AtomicBool::new(false),
   212	            task: Task::current().unwrap(),
   213	        });
   214	        let waiter = Self {
   215	            waker: waker.clone(),
   216	        };
   217	        (waiter, waker)
   218	    }
   219	
   220	    /// Waits until the waiter is woken up by calling [`Waker::wake_up`] on the associated
   221	    /// [`Waker`].
   222	    ///
   223	    /// This method returns immediately if the waiter has been woken since the end of the last call
   224	    /// to this method (or since the waiter was created, if this method has not been called
   225	    /// before). Otherwise, it puts the current thread to sleep until the waiter is woken up.
   226	    pub fn wait(&self) {
   227	        self.waker.do_wait();
   228	    }
   229	
   230	    /// Gets the associated [`Waker`] of the current waiter.
   231	    pub fn waker(&self) -> Arc<Waker> {
   232	        self.waker.clone()
   233	    }
   234	}
   235	
   236	impl Drop for Waiter {
   237	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   238	        // When dropping the waiter, we need to close the waker to ensure that if someone wants to
   239	        // wake up the waiter afterwards, they will perform a no-op.
   240	        self.waker.close();
   241	    }
   242	}
   243	
   244	impl Waker {
   245	    /// Wakes up the associated [`Waiter`].
   246	    ///
   247	    /// This method returns `true` if the waiter is woken by this call. It returns `false` if the
   248	    /// waiter has already been woken by a previous call to the method, or if the waiter has been
   249	    /// dropped.
   250	    ///
   251	    /// Note that if this method returns `true`, it implies that the wake event will be properly
   252	    /// delivered, _or_ that the waiter will be dropped after being woken. It's up to the caller to
   253	    /// handle the latter case properly to avoid missing the wake event.
   254	    pub fn wake_up(&self) -> bool {
   255	        if self.has_woken.swap(true, Ordering::Release) {
   256	            return false;
   257	        }
   258	        scheduler::unpark_target(self.task.clone());
   259	
   260	        true
   261	    }
   262	
   263	    fn do_wait(&self) {
   264	        let has_woken = &self.has_woken;
   265	        while !has_woken.swap(false, Ordering::Acquire) {
   266	            scheduler::park_current(has_woken);
   267	        }
   268	    }
   269	
   270	    fn close(&self) {
   271	        // This must use `Ordering::Acquire`, although we do not care about the return value. See
   272	        // the memory order explanation at the top of the file for details.
   273	        let _ = self.has_woken.swap(true, Ordering::Acquire);
   274	    }
   275	}

     3	use super::SyscallReturn;
     4	use crate::{
     5	    ipc::{
     6	        semaphore::system_v::{
     7	            sem_set::{check_sem, create_sem_set, create_sem_set_with_id, SEMMSL},
     8	            PermissionMode,
     9	        },
    10	        IpcFlags,
    11	    },
    12	    prelude::*,
    13	};
    14	
    15	pub fn sys_semget(key: i32, nsems: i32, semflags: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    16	    if nsems < 0 || nsems as usize > SEMMSL {
    17	        return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
    18	    }
    19	    if key < 0 {
    20	        return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
    21	    }
    22	
    23	    let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(semflags as u32);
    24	    let mode: u16 = (semflags as u32 & 0x1FF) as u16;
    25	    let nsems = nsems as usize;
    26	    let credentials = ctx.posix_thread.credentials();
    27	
    28	    debug!(
    29	        "[sys_semget] key = {}, nsems = {}, flags = {:?}",
    30	        key, nsems, semflags
    31	    );
    32	
    33	    // Create a new semaphore set directly
    34	    const IPC_NEW: i32 = 0;
    35	    if key == IPC_NEW {
    36	        if nsems == 0 {
    37	            return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
    38	        }
    39	        return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(
    40	            create_sem_set(nsems, mode, credentials)? as isize,
    41	        ));
    42	    }
    43	
    44	    // Get a semaphore set, and create if necessary
    45	    match check_sem(
    46	        key,
    47	        Some(nsems),
    48	        PermissionMode::ALTER | PermissionMode::READ,
    49	    ) {
    50	        Ok(_) => {
    51	            if flags.contains(IpcFlags::IPC_CREAT | IpcFlags::IPC_EXCL) {
    52	                return_errno!(Errno::EEXIST);
    53	            }
    54	        }
    55	        Err(err) => {
    56	            let need_create = err.error() == Errno::ENOENT && flags.contains(IpcFlags::IPC_CREAT);
    57	            if !need_create {
    58	                return Err(err);
    59	            }
    60	            if nsems == 0 {
    61	                return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
    62	            }
    63	
    64	            create_sem_set_with_id(key, nsems, mode, credentials)?
    65	        }
    66	    };
    67	
    68	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(key as isize))
    69	}

    18	/// A timeout, represented in one of the two ways.
    19	pub enum Timeout {
    20	    /// The timeout is reached _after_ the `Duration` time is elapsed.
    21	    After(Duration),
    22	    /// The timeout is reached _when_ the clock's time is equal to `Duration`.
    23	    When(Duration),
    24	}
    25	
    26	/// A timer with periodic functionality.
    27	///
    28	/// Setting the timer will trigger a callback function upon expiration of
    29	/// the set time. To enable its periodic functionality, users should set
    30	/// its `interval` field with [`Timer::set_interval`]. By doing this,
    31	/// the timer will use the interval time to configure a new timing after expiration.
    32	pub struct Timer {
    33	    interval: SpinLock<Duration>,
    34	    timer_manager: Arc<TimerManager>,
    35	    registered_callback: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>,
    36	    timer_callback: SpinLock<Weak<TimerCallback>>,
    37	}
    38	
    39	impl Timer {
    40	    /// Create a `Timer` instance from a [`TimerManager`].
    41	    /// This timer will be managed by the `TimerManager`.
    42	    ///
    43	    /// Note that if the callback instructions involves sleep, users should put these instructions
    44	    /// into something like `WorkQueue` to avoid sleeping during system timer interruptions.
    45	    fn new<F>(registered_callback: F, timer_manager: Arc<TimerManager>) -> Arc<Self>
    46	    where
    47	        F: Fn() + Send + Sync + 'static,
    48	    {
    49	        Arc::new(Self {
    50	            interval: SpinLock::new(Duration::ZERO),
    51	            timer_manager,
    52	            registered_callback: Box::new(registered_callback),
    53	            timer_callback: SpinLock::new(Weak::default()),
    54	        })
    55	    }
    56	
    57	    /// Set the interval time for this timer.
    58	    /// The timer will be reset with the interval time upon expiration.
    59	    pub fn set_interval(&self, interval: Duration) {
    60	        *self.interval.disable_irq().lock() = interval;
    61	    }
    62	
    63	    /// Cancel the current timer's set timeout callback.
    64	    pub fn cancel(&self) {
    65	        let timer_callback = self.timer_callback.disable_irq().lock();
    66	        if let Some(timer_callback) = timer_callback.upgrade() {
    67	            timer_callback.cancel();
    68	        }
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    /// Set the timer with a timeout.
    72	    ///
    73	    /// The registered callback function of this timer will be invoked
    74	    /// when reaching timeout. If the timer has a valid interval, this timer
    75	    /// will be set again with the interval when reaching timeout.
    76	    pub fn set_timeout(self: &Arc<Self>, timeout: Timeout) {
    77	        let expired_time = match timeout {
    78	            Timeout::After(timeout) => {
    79	                let now = self.timer_manager.clock.read_time();
    80	                now + timeout
    81	            }
    82	            Timeout::When(timeout) => timeout,
    83	        };
    84	
    85	        let timer_weak = Arc::downgrade(self);
    86	        let new_timer_callback = Arc::new(TimerCallback::new(
    87	            expired_time,
    88	            Box::new(move || interval_timer_callback(&timer_weak)),
    89	        ));
    90	
    91	        let mut timer_callback = self.timer_callback.disable_irq().lock();
    92	        if let Some(timer_callback) = timer_callback.upgrade() {
    93	            timer_callback.cancel();
    94	        }
    95	        *timer_callback = Arc::downgrade(&new_timer_callback);
    96	        self.timer_manager.insert(new_timer_callback);
    97	    }
    98	
    99	    /// Return the current expired time of this timer.
   100	    pub fn expired_time(&self) -> Duration {
   101	        let timer_callback = self.timer_callback.disable_irq().lock().upgrade();
   102	        timer_callback.map_or(Duration::ZERO, |timer_callback| timer_callback.expired_time)
   103	    }
   104	
   105	    /// Return the remain time to expiration of this timer.
   106	    ///
   107	    /// If the timer has not been set, this method
   108	    /// will return `Duration::ZERO`.
   109	    pub fn remain(&self) -> Duration {
   110	        let now = self.timer_manager.clock.read_time();
   111	        let expired_time = self.expired_time();
   112	        if expired_time > now {
   113	            expired_time - now
   114	        } else {
   115	            Duration::ZERO
   116	        }
   117	    }
   118	
   119	    /// Return a reference to the [`TimerManager`] which manages
   120	    /// the current timer.
   121	    pub fn timer_manager(&self) -> &Arc<TimerManager> {
   122	        &self.timer_manager
   123	    }
   124	
   125	    /// Returns the interval time of the current timer.
   126	    pub fn interval(&self) -> Duration {
   127	        *self.interval.disable_irq().lock()
   128	    }
   129	}
   130	
   131	fn interval_timer_callback(timer: &Weak<Timer>) {
   132	    let Some(timer) = timer.upgrade() else {
   133	        return;
   134	    };
   135	
   136	    (timer.registered_callback)();
   137	    let interval = timer.interval.disable_irq().lock();
   138	    if *interval != Duration::ZERO {
   139	        timer.set_timeout(Timeout::After(*interval));
   140	    }
   141	}
   142	
   143	/// `TimerManager` is used to create timers and manage their expiries. It holds a clock and can
   144	/// create [`Timer`]s based on this clock.
   145	///
   146	/// These created `Timer`s will hold an `Arc` pointer to this manager, hence this manager
   147	/// will be actually dropped after all the created timers have been dropped.
   148	pub struct TimerManager {
   149	    clock: Arc<dyn Clock>,
   150	    timer_callbacks: SpinLock<BinaryHeap<Arc<TimerCallback>>>,
   151	}
   152	
   153	impl TimerManager {
   154	    /// Create a `TimerManager` instance from a clock.
   155	    pub fn new(clock: Arc<dyn Clock>) -> Arc<Self> {
   156	        Arc::new(Self {
   157	            clock,
   158	            timer_callbacks: SpinLock::new(BinaryHeap::new()),
   159	        })
   160	    }
   161	
   162	    fn insert(&self, timer_callback: Arc<TimerCallback>) {
   163	        self.timer_callbacks
   164	            .disable_irq()
   165	            .lock()
   166	            .push(timer_callback);
   167	    }
   168	
   169	    /// Check the managed timers, and if any have timed out,
   170	    /// call the corresponding callback functions.
   171	    pub fn process_expired_timers(&self) {
   172	        let callbacks = {
   173	            let mut timeout_list = self.timer_callbacks.disable_irq().lock();
   174	            if timeout_list.len() == 0 {
   175	                return;
   176	            }
   177	
   178	            let mut callbacks = Vec::new();
   179	            let current_time = self.clock.read_time();
   180	            while let Some(t) = timeout_list.peek() {
   181	                if t.is_cancelled() {
   182	                    // Just ignore the cancelled callback
   183	                    timeout_list.pop();
   184	                } else if t.expired_time <= current_time {
   185	                    callbacks.push(timeout_list.pop().unwrap());
   186	                } else {
   187	                    break;
   188	                }
   189	            }
   190	            callbacks
   191	        };
   192	
   193	        for callback in callbacks {
   194	            (callback.callback)();
   195	        }
   196	    }
   197	
   198	    /// Create an [`Timer`], which will be managed by this `TimerManager`.
   199	    pub fn create_timer<F>(self: &Arc<Self>, function: F) -> Arc<Timer>
   200	    where
   201	        F: Fn() + Send + Sync + 'static,
   202	    {
   203	        Timer::new(function, self.clone())
   204	    }
   205	}
   206	
   207	/// A `TimerCallback` can be used to execute a timer callback function.
   208	struct TimerCallback {
   209	    expired_time: Duration,
   210	    callback: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>,
   211	    is_cancelled: AtomicBool,
   212	}
   213	
   214	impl TimerCallback {
   215	    /// Create an instance of `TimerCallback`.
   216	    fn new(timeout: Duration, callback: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>) -> Self {
   217	        Self {
   218	            expired_time: timeout,
   219	            callback,
   220	            is_cancelled: AtomicBool::new(false),
   221	        }

codex
I found several concrete failure scenarios: duplicate entries in a single `semop` array are not validated cumulatively, `GETNCNT`/`GETZCNT` overcount from an empty queue, and timeout cleanup removes by process id instead of by the specific pending operation. I’m checking whether any of these are precluded by caller validation before emitting JSON.

#!/usr/bin/env bash

# SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

#
# assemble_review.sh — deterministic merge of persona comment fragments into the review file body.
# No LLM judgement here:
# group by persona in a fixed order,
# sort by file then line within each group,
# drop exact duplicates *within a persona*,
# write the frontmatter,
# and leave a <!-- SUMMARY --> placeholder for the orchestrator's LLM summary step.
#
# Usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>
#   meta-file: lines `mode=`, `base=` (diff) or `files=` (files), `head=`,
#              `branch=`, `date=`, `title=`
#   frag-dir:  one <persona>.json per persona, each a JSON array of comments:
#       {"file","line","grounding","severity","problem","fix","diff"}
#   --overwrite: replace <output-file> if it already exists (otherwise fail).
set -euo pipefail

overwrite=0
pos=()
for a in "$@"; do
    case "$a" in
        --overwrite) overwrite=1 ;;
        --*) echo "assemble_review.sh: unknown flag: $a" >&2; exit 2 ;;
        *) pos+=("$a") ;;
    esac
done
if [[ ${#pos[@]} -ne 3 ]]; then
    echo "usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>" >&2
    exit 2
fi
meta="${pos[0]}"; fragdir="${pos[1]}"; out="${pos[2]}"

# Refuse to clobber an existing review unless --overwrite (design §3).
if [[ -e "$out" && $overwrite -eq 0 ]]; then
    echo "assemble_review.sh: refusing to overwrite existing $out (pass --overwrite)" >&2
    exit 1
fi

python3 - "$meta" "$fragdir" "$out" <<'PY'
import json, os, re, sys
meta_path, fragdir, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]

# A guideline short-name is a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`) and
# renders as code — a citable rule. A bug's grounding is a short plain-language
# description (e.g. "Off by one") and renders as prose, so it never reads as a rule name.
KEBAB = re.compile(r"[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*")
def grounding_tag(g):
    return "`%s`" % g if KEBAB.fullmatch(g) else g

meta = {}
for line in open(meta_path):
    line = line.strip()
    if "=" in line:
        k, v = line.split("=", 1)
        meta[k] = v

# Fixed persona order -> review section title.
# "Correctness" is the Development persona's section (a bare "## Development" reads oddly; see the design doc).
ORDER = [("maintainability", "Maintainability"),
         ("development",      "Correctness"),
         ("security",         "Security"),
         ("hardware",         "Hardware"),
         ("documentation",    "Documentation")]

def load(persona):
    p = os.path.join(fragdir, persona + ".json")
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        return []          # persona not activated / did not run: legitimately absent
    # An EXISTING fragment that is unparseable or not a JSON array means a pass
    # produced broken output. Failing closed (rather than dropping it) is essential:
    # this skill is recall-first, so a silently-discarded fragment is a silent
    # recall hole the benchmark/orchestrator would never notice.
    try:
        data = json.load(open(p))
    except Exception as e:
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: unparseable fragment {p}: {e}\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    if not isinstance(data, list):
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: fragment {p} is not a JSON array of comments\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    return data

L = ["---",
     f"date: {meta.get('date','')}",
     f"mode: {meta.get('mode','diff')}"]
if meta.get("base"):
    L.append(f"base: {meta['base']}")
if meta.get("files"):
    L.append(f"files: {meta['files']}")
L += [f"head: {meta.get('head','')}",
      f"branch: {meta.get('branch','')}"]
if meta.get("title"):
    # json.dumps yields a valid YAML double-quoted scalar (escapes embedded quotes).
    L.append("title: " + json.dumps(meta["title"]))
L += ["---", "", "# Summary", "", "<!-- SUMMARY -->", ""]

counts = {}
for persona, title in ORDER:
    # Dedup is per-persona: identical comments from two different personas are both kept,
    # in their own sections (design §4).
    seen = set()
    uniq = []
    for c in load(persona):
        # "Exact" duplicate: identical in EVERY field (a normalized full-object key),
        # so two comments that differ in grounding, severity, or diff are both kept.
        # A partial key would collapse distinct findings — a recall hole.
        key = json.dumps(c, sort_keys=True)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        uniq.append(c)
    counts[persona] = len(uniq)
    if not uniq:
        continue
    # Sort by file, then line, then a stable text key
    # so co-located comments have a deterministic order regardless of pass output order.
    uniq.sort(key=lambda c: (str(c.get("file", "")),
                             int(c.get("line", 0) or 0),
                             str(c.get("grounding", "")),
                             str(c.get("problem", ""))))
    L += [f"## {title}", ""]
    for c in uniq:
        loc = "`%s`" % c.get("file", "?")
        if c.get("line"):
            loc += " line %s" % c["line"]
        L += [f"### {loc}", ""]
        diff = c.get("diff")
        if diff:
            L.append("> ```diff")
            L += ["> " + dl for dl in str(diff).splitlines()]
            L += ["> ```", ""]
        L.append("%s (%s): %s" % (grounding_tag(c.get("grounding", "issue")),
                                  c.get("severity", "major"),
                                  (c.get("problem", "") or "").strip()))
        L += ["", "**Fix.** %s" % (c.get("fix", "") or "").strip(), ""]

open(out, "w").write("\n".join(L).rstrip() + "\n")
sys.stderr.write("assemble: " + ", ".join(f"{p}={counts[p]}" for p, _ in ORDER) + "\n")
PY

/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '1,260p' .agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/pass_contract.md" in /tmp/tmp.rVvzXUSw1Z/wt1
 succeeded in 0ms:
# Pass contract

You are a reviewer applying the persona guideline(s) included below to the change or files under review
(the **REVIEW INPUT** at the very end of this prompt).
Find as many real defects as possible within the included persona(s)' remit
— runtime correctness for Development,
security/soundness for Security,
ABI/hardware for Hardware,
doc style/currency for Documentation,
and structure/process for Maintainability
— without inventing issues; a false alarm is a real cost.

For each persona block below,
work that persona's concerns in the order its file gives.
For each candidate rule,
read its one-line gist first
and drill into the full rule (its linked subsections) only on a suspected violation.
When a rule has `#### Steps`,
use those steps as the review procedure for deciding whether the code violates that rule.
Emit a JSON comment object only for a confirmed violation;
emit nothing for compliant code.
Stay within the remit of the persona(s) you are given.

Each persona searches only for defects whose failure belongs to that persona.
Do not run a general bug sweep from every persona.
When another persona is the clear natural owner,
do not duplicate that investigation here.
For example,
Maintainability should inspect design shape, readability, naming, layout,
and commit hygiene;
it should not trace runtime permission semantics, Linux/POSIX behavior,
wrong predicates, or data-flow edge cases unless they are evidence of a
maintainability rule violation.

Within each included persona's owned failure modes,
reason about the code even when no explicit guideline names the issue.
Examples include off-by-one and reachable panic for Development,
input-validation or permission-boundary flaws for Security,
ABI/alignment hazards for Hardware,
navigation or currency defects for Documentation,
and structural or process defects for Maintainability.
Ground each non-guideline finding in a short plain-language description of the defect
("Off by one", "Use after free", "Reachable panic", …)
— not the bare word `bug`, and not a coined hyphenated short-name,
which would read as a guideline.
Never stay silent about a real defect that belongs to the included persona
because "no guideline covers it".

Be **adversarial**:
before dismissing a suspected in-scope defect as safe,
state the concrete input or interleaving that would trigger it.
Report an in-scope defect unless you can show that case cannot happen.
"It looks fine" is not a verdict.

The REVIEW INPUT is the unit of review;
you MAY read surrounding code in the working tree for extra context.

## Output

Output **only** a JSON array of comment objects (no prose around it):

```json
[{"file":"path/relative/to/repo.rs","line":42,"persona":"development","grounding":"lock-ordering","severity":"major",
  "problem":"`foo()` takes `b.lock()` while already holding `a.lock()`, the reverse of the `a`-before-`b` order elsewhere — a deadlock",
  "fix":"take `a.lock()` before `b.lock()` here too, matching the rest of the module",
  "diff":"the few relevant lines (a diff hunk, or source lines in files mode)"}]
```

- `persona` — which persona section the comment belongs to (`maintainability`, `development`, `security`, `hardware`, `documentation`);
  used to file the comment under the right section.
  In a single-persona (fan-out) pass it is always that persona.
- `grounding` — what the comment rests on, in one of two forms kept visually distinct:
  when you **cite a guideline**, its short-name
  — a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`), rendered as code;
  when you report a **bug no guideline covers**, a short plain-language description of the defect
  (e.g. "Off by one", "Use after free", "Incorrect cleanup"), rendered as prose.
  Do not coin a hyphenated pseudo-short-name for a bug
  — that reads as a guideline
  — and never use the bare word `bug`,
  which says nothing the reader cannot already see.
- `severity` — **required**,
  one of `critical` (must fix) / `major` (should fix) / `minor` (worth fixing) / `nit` (optional or stylistic).
- `problem` and `fix` are **both required**
  — every comment proposes a remedy.
  They are posted as GitHub-flavored Markdown,
  so wrap every code identifier, path, type, function or variable name, and literal value in backticks

3. Require an explicit lock-order comment when the order is non-obvious or spans multiple objects or subsystems.
4. Report any path that can acquire the same lock pair in the opposite order, including error paths and callbacks invoked while locked.

See also:
PR [#2942](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/2942).

### Never do I/O or blocking operations while holding a spinlock (`no-io-under-spinlock`) {#no-io-under-spinlock}

Holding a spinlock while performing I/O
or blocking operations is a deadlock hazard.
Use a sleeping mutex or restructure
to drop the lock first.

```rust
// Good — spinlock dropped before I/O
let data = {
    let guard = self.state.lock(); // state: SpinLock<...>
    guard.pending_data.clone()
};
self.device.write(&data)?;

// Bad — I/O while holding spinlock
let guard = self.state.lock(); // state: SpinLock<...>
self.device.write(&guard.pending_data)?;
```

#### Steps

1. Find critical sections protected by spinlocks or IRQ-disabling guards in the changed code.
2. Inspect calls made while the guard is live, including trait methods and logging paths.
3. Flag I/O, sleeping, waiting, scheduling, user-memory access, or blocking allocation under the guard.
4. Require dropping the guard before blocking work, or using a sleeping mutex when the work must stay protected.

See also:
PR [#925](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/925).

### Do not use atomics casually (`careful-atomics`) {#careful-atomics}

When multiple atomic fields
must be updated in concert, use a lock.
Only use atomics when a single value
is genuinely independent.

```rust
// Good — a lock protects correlated fields
struct Stats {
# Efficiency

### Avoid O(n) algorithms on hot paths (`no-linear-hot-paths`) {#no-linear-hot-paths}

System call dispatch, scheduler enqueue,
and frequent query operations
must not introduce O(n) complexity
where n is a quantity that can be large
(number of processes, number of file descriptors, etc.).
Demand sub-linear alternatives.

```rust
// Bad — O(n) scan on every enqueue
fn select_cpu(&self, cpus: &[CpuState]) -> CpuId {
    cpus.iter()
        .min_by_key(|c| c.load())
        .expect("at least one CPU")
        .id()
}

// Good — maintain a priority queue
// so selection is O(log n)
fn select_cpu(&self) -> CpuId {
    self.cpu_heap.peek().expect("at least one CPU").id()
}
```

#### Steps

1. Identify changed code on frequent paths: syscalls, scheduling, page faults, networking, descriptor lookup, or interrupt-adjacent work.
2. For each loop, scan, sort, allocation, or traversal there, name `n` and whether it can be large.
3. Require an indexed, cached, queued, tree-based, or otherwise sub-linear design when the operation repeats on a hot path over large `n`.
4. Accept a linear operation only when the bound is small and explicit, or when measured evidence shows it is not on a hot path.

See also:
PR [#1790](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/1790).

### Minimize unnecessary copies and allocations (`minimize-copies`) {#minimize-copies}

Extra data copies —
serializing to a stack buffer before writing,
cloning an `Arc` when a `&` reference suffices,
collecting into a `Vec` when an iterator would do —
should be avoided.

```rust
// Bad — unnecessary Arc::clone
fn process(&self, stream: Arc<DmaStream>) {
    let s = stream.clone();
    s.sync();
}

// Good — borrow when ownership is not needed
fn process(&self, stream: &DmaStream) {
    stream.sync();
}
```

#### Steps

1. Search the diff for `clone`, `to_vec`, `collect`, temporary buffers, serialization steps, and ownership changes from references to owned values.
2. Decide whether the new owner is needed for lifetime, mutation, sharing across tasks, or storage beyond the call.
3. Require borrowing, slicing, iterator chaining, or moving an existing value when ownership or allocation is not necessary.
4. Keep copies that cross protection, DMA, userspace, or lifetime boundaries only when the boundary makes the copy part of the correctness contract.

See also:
PR [#2582](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/2582)
and [#2725](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/2725).

### No premature optimization without evidence (`no-premature-optimization`) {#no-premature-optimization}

Performance optimizations
must be justified with data.
Introducing complexity
to solve a non-existent problem is rejected.
If you claim a change improves performance,
show the numbers.

#### Steps

1. Identify changes whose main justification is speed, memory use, cache behavior, or reduced instruction count.
2. Ask for benchmark data, profiling output, or a concrete complexity argument that matches the affected workload.
3. Compare the performance gain against added branches, unsafe code, synchronization complexity, API complexity, and maintenance cost.
4. Prefer the simpler implementation when the optimization lacks evidence or targets an unimportant path.

# Summary
