## Maintainability

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> ```

`imperative-subject` (nit): The subject is imperative and within the length limit, but the syscall identifier `semop` is not wrapped in backticks as required by the commit-message convention.

**Fix.** Keep the imperative subject and wrap the syscall identifier `semop` in backticks.

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> 
> val: SpinLock<i32> -> val: i32
> sem_buf: SemBuf -> sops: Vec<SemBuf>
> sem_op(&SemBuf) -> sem_op(Vec<SemBuf>)
> ```

`refactor-then-feature` (major): This commit combines the representation and locking refactor (`Semaphore` loses its per-value lock and `SemaphoreSet` gains `SemSetInner`) with the new multi-operation `semop` execution and wait queues. A failure in the new semantics cannot be isolated from the storage rewrite during review or bisection.

**Fix.** Split this into an earlier behavior-preserving refactor of `Semaphore` and `SemaphoreSet`, followed by a separate commit implementing atomic `semop` and its queueing and wakeup behavior.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 43

> ```diff
> pub enum Status {
>     Normal = 0,
>     Pending = 1,
>     Removed = 2,
> }
> 
> pub struct PendingOp {
>     sops: Vec<SemBuf>,
>     status: Arc<AtomicStatus>,
>     waker: Option<Arc<Waker>>,
>     pid: Pid,
> }
> ```

`narrow-visibility` (minor): The new `Status`, `PendingOp`, and all four `PendingOp` methods are `pub`, but their only current consumers are the sibling `sem.rs` and `sem_set.rs` modules. This exposes implementation-only wait state through the crate-wide interface.

**Fix.** Use `pub(super)` for `Status`, `PendingOp`, and the cross-module methods, leaving members private wherever a sibling does not need them.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 156

> ```diff
> let (alter, dupsop) = get_sops_flags(&pending_op);
> if dupsop {
>     warn!("Found duplicate sop");
> }
> ```

`bool-names` (minor): The new booleans `alter` and `dupsop` do not read as assertions, and `dupsop` is an unexplained abbreviation. At `if alter`, the reader must infer that it means the operation contains a nonzero `sem_op`.

**Fix.** Use names such as `has_altering_op` and `has_duplicate_sem_num`, and return a small named type if both flags remain part of the helper result.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 182

> ```diff
> // Prepare to wait
> let status = pending_op.status.clone();
> let (waiter, waker) = Waiter::new_pair();
> 
> // Check if timeout exists to avoid calling `Arc::clone()`
> if let Some(timeout) = timeout {
> ```

`single-responsibility` (minor): The changed `sem_op` handles operation classification and execution, waiter and timer construction, queue insertion, lock lifetime, timeout cleanup, and final status decoding in one function. The syscall-level flow is buried under synchronization mechanics.

**Fix.** Extract the blocking lifecycle into a helper such as `wait_for_pending_op` that owns waiter and timer setup, enqueueing, cancellation, and status-to-error conversion; leave `sem_op` to coordinate lookup and atomic execution.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 229

> ```diff
> /// Update pending const and alter operations, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L1029>
> /// Look for pending alter operations that can be completed, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L949>
> /// Wakeup all wait for zero tasks, ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/ipc/sem.c#L893>
> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (nit): The added rustdoc summaries use imperative fragments (`Update`, `Look`, `Wakeup`, `Iter`, `Perform`) instead of concise third-person summaries for functions, so rustdoc listings read inconsistently.

**Fix.** Rewrite the summaries with third-person verbs, for example `Updates pending operations`, `Looks for completable alter operations`, `Wakes pending zero operations`, and `Performs an atomic semaphore operation`.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 300

> ```diff
> /// Iter the sops and return the flags (alter, dupsop)
> fn get_sops_flags(pending_op: &PendingOp) -> (bool, bool) {
> ```

`backtick-identifiers` (nit): The added `///` comments refer to code identifiers and results such as `sops`, `alter`, `dupsop`, `Ok(true)`, and `Err(err)` without rustdoc backticks, rendering API terms as ordinary prose.

**Fix.** Wrap code identifiers in backticks and use rustdoc links where applicable; for example, document `get_sops_flags` as `Returns whether the operation alters a semaphore and whether it repeats a semaphore number.`

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 59

> ```diff
> pub(super) pending_const: LinkedList<PendingOp>,
> 
> pub fn pending_const_count(&self, sem_num: u16) -> usize {
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): `pending_const`, `pending_const_count`, and `wake_const_ops` describe the queue as `const`, while its meaning is `sem_op == 0` and nearby code calls the same case `zero`. `const` is easily read as a compile-time constant rather than a zero-wait operation.

**Fix.** Rename the queue and APIs consistently to `pending_zero`, `pending_zero_count`, and `wake_zero_ops`, and update the related comments.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 143

> ```diff
> pub fn get<T>(&self, sem_num: usize, func: &dyn Fn(&Semaphore) -> T) -> Result<T> {
>     let inner = self.inner();
>     Ok(func(
>         inner.sems.get(sem_num).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?,
>     ))
> }
> ```

`information-hiding` (major): The new public `get` method exposes the internal `Semaphore` type and a storage-oriented callback to callers. `sys_semctl` must import `Semaphore` and define `sem_val` and `sem_pid` callbacks just to read two fields, so representation changes become cross-module API changes.

**Fix.** Add dedicated operations on `SemaphoreSet`, such as `val(sem_num)` and `latest_modified_pid(sem_num)`, and keep `Semaphore` and its field accessors private to the semaphore implementation.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 158

> ```diff
> pub fn update_ctime(&self) {
>     self.sem_ctime.store(
>         RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time().as_secs(),
>         Ordering::Relaxed,
>     );
> }
> 
> pub fn update_otime(&self) {
> ```

`narrow-visibility` (minor): The timestamp mutators `update_ctime` and `update_otime` are exposed as `pub`, even though current call sites are inside `SemaphoreSet` and `sem.rs`. Unrelated code can therefore mutate IPC metadata without going through the operation that owns the state.

**Fix.** Make `update_ctime` private and `update_otime` `pub(super)`, or keep timestamp updates behind the corresponding semaphore-set operations.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 154

> ```diff
> // TODO: Support permission check
> warn!("Semaphore operation doesn't support permission check now");
> ```

`log-levels` (minor): This `warn!` executes on every `semop`, including successful operations, so normal syscall traffic floods warning logs with an expected unsupported-feature message.

**Fix.** Use `debug!` for routine diagnostics, or emit a warning only when the call actually takes an exceptional permission-handling path.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 173

> ```diff
> for wake_op in wake_queue {
>     wake_op.set_status(Status::Normal);
>     if let Some(waker) = wake_op.waker {
>         waker.wake_up();
>     }
> }
> ```

`no-io-under-spinlock` (major): `waker.wake_up()` calls the scheduler's `unpark_target` while `inner` is still held as a `SpinLock`. This performs scheduler queue work in the spinlock critical section; the same pattern also appears in `SemaphoreSet::setval` and `SemaphoreSet::drop`.

**Fix.** Detach the wake list, release `inner` and any other spinlock guards, then set statuses and call `wake_up()` outside the critical section.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 190

> ```diff
> let jiffies_timer = JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER.get().unwrap().create_timer(move || {
>     waker.wake_up();
> });
> jiffies_timer.set_timeout(Timeout::After(timeout));
> ```

Timer lifetime (major): `jiffies_timer` is the only strong `Arc<Timer>` and is dropped when the `if let` block ends. The timer manager retains only a callback containing a `Weak<Timer>`, so the callback cannot upgrade the timer and never calls `waker.wake_up()`. A blocked `semtimedop` therefore does not time out.

**Fix.** Keep the timer `Arc` alive until the waiter finishes, and cancel it when the operation completes before the deadline.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 292

> ```diff
> while let Some(const_op) = cursor.current() {
>     if let Ok(true) = perform_atomic_semop(sems, const_op) {
>         wake_queue.append(&mut cursor.remove_current_as_list().unwrap());
>     } else {
>         cursor.move_next();
>     }
> }
> ```

Stuck pending operation (major): `wake_const_ops` treats every `Err` from `perform_atomic_semop` as if the operation merely still needs to wait. A request such as `[{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 0 }, { sem_num: 999, sem_op: 0 }]` can be queued because the first operation blocks; after semaphore `0` reaches zero, the later `EFBIG` is ignored and the request remains queued forever.

**Fix.** Validate every semaphore index and other terminal errors before enqueueing a request, and remove or report queued operations that fail with a terminal error instead of silently treating all errors as waiting.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 329

> ```diff
> for op in pending_op.sops_iter() {
>     let sem = sems.get(op.sem_num as usize).ok_or(Errno::EFBIG)?;
>     let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(op.sem_flags as u32);
>     result = sem.val();
> 
>     result += i32::from(op.sem_op);
> }
> ```

Incorrect cumulative state (major): `result = sem.val()` reloads the original value for every `sop`, so repeated operations on one semaphore are not evaluated sequentially. For example, with `sem0 = 0`, `[{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 1 }, { sem_num: 0, sem_op: -1 }]` incorrectly blocks, while multiple positive operations can commit a final value above `SEMVMX`.

**Fix.** Track tentative values or accumulated deltas per semaphore during validation, then commit the complete operation only after every `sop` succeeds.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 353

> ```diff
> if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
>     todo!()
> }
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): `SEM_UNDO` is a valid user-supplied flag, but the new multi-operation path executes `todo!()` when it encounters it. A successful call such as `semop([{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 1, sem_flags: SEM_UNDO }])` can therefore panic the kernel.

**Fix.** Implement `SEM_UNDO` bookkeeping or reject the flag with an error such as `ENOSYS` before the operation can reach this path; do not use `todo!()` for user-controlled input.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 84

> ```diff
> let pending_const = &inner.pending_const;
> let mut count = 1;
> for i in pending_const.iter() {
>     for sem_buf in i.sops_iter() {
>         if sem_buf.sem_num() == sem_num {
>             count += 1;
>         }
>     }
> }
> ```

Wrong waiter count (major): `pending_const_count` initializes `count` to `1`, so `SEM_GETZCNT` reports one waiter even when the queue is empty. It also increments once per matching `SemBuf`, so one pending atomic request with duplicate zero operations is counted multiple times instead of once.

**Fix.** Initialize the count to `0` and count pending requests, not matching `SemBuf` entries; a request should contribute at most once for the queried semaphore.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 98

> ```diff

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '260,620p' /tmp/tmp.rVvzXUSw1Z/wt1.on.md" in /tmp/tmp.rVvzXUSw1Z/wt1
 succeeded in 0ms:
> ```diff
> let pending_alter = &inner.pending_alter;
> let mut count = 1;
> for i in pending_alter.iter() {
>     for sem_buf in i.sops_iter() {
>         if sem_buf.sem_num() == sem_num {
>             count += 1;
>         }
>     }
> }
> ```

Wrong waiter count (major): `pending_alter_count` has the same off-by-one and per-entry counting errors, so `SEM_GETNCNT` returns `1` with no pending operations and overcounts atomic requests containing multiple matching operations.

**Fix.** Start from `0` and count each pending request once for the semaphore on which it is actually waiting.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83

> ```diff
> IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETZCNT => {
>     let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
>         Ok(sem_set.pending_const_count(semnum as u16))
>     })?;
> }
> ```

Invalid semaphore index accepted (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT` cast the user-supplied `semnum` to `u16` and call count helpers that never validate membership. `semnum = nsems` succeeds instead of returning `EINVAL`, and `semnum = 65536` wraps to semaphore `0`.

**Fix.** Validate the original `semnum` as a `usize` against `sem_set.nsems()` before counting, and avoid the narrowing `u16` conversion.

## Security

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 153

> ```diff
> 153     // TODO: Support permission check
> 154     warn!("Semaphore operation doesn't support permission check now");
> ...
> 167     if perform_atomic_semop(&mut inner.sems, &mut pending_op)? {
> ```

Permission bypass (critical): `sem_op()` only logs that permission checks are unsupported and then continues to apply or enqueue the operation. A caller that knows a valid `sem_id` can therefore perform `sem_op != 0` operations on a semaphore set without `ALTER` permission, modifying IPC state owned by another user.

**Fix.** Before calling `perform_atomic_semop()`, compute the required `PermissionMode` from `sops` and reject unauthorized callers. For example, require `PermissionMode::ALTER` for any `SemBuf` with `sem_op != 0` and `PermissionMode::READ` for wait-for-zero operations, then call the same permission machinery used by `semctl` instead of only warning.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 353

> ```diff
> 328         let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(op.sem_flags as u32);
> ...
> 352         if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
> 353             todo!()
> 354         }
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): A userspace `SemBuf` can set `sem_flags` to include `SEM_UNDO`; once the operation is otherwise satisfiable, `perform_atomic_semop()` reaches `todo!()`, which panics in kernel code. For example, `sem_op = 0`, `sem_num = 0`, and `SEM_UNDO` on a zero-valued semaphore reaches this path.

**Fix.** Remove the userspace-reachable `todo!()`: either implement `SEM_UNDO` handling or reject unsupported `SEM_UNDO` requests during syscall-boundary validation before any operation can be applied or enqueued.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83

> ```diff
> 81         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETZCNT => {
> 82             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
> 83                 Ok(sem_set.pending_const_count(semnum as u16))
> 84             })?;
> ...
> 88         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETNCNT => {
> 89             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
> 90                 Ok(sem_set.pending_alter_count(semnum as u16))
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT` cast the user-supplied `semnum` to `u16` instead of validating it against `nsems`. A hostile caller can pass `semnum = 65536` and have it silently treated as semaphore `0`, so an out-of-range syscall argument is accepted and used internally.

**Fix.** Keep `semnum` as `usize` and validate it with the target `SemaphoreSet` before counting, returning `EINVAL` for `semnum >= nsems`; do the same for both `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semop.rs` line 63

> ```diff
> 60     let user_space = ctx.get_user_space();
> 61     let mut semops = Vec::with_capacity(nsops);
> 62     for i in 0..nsops {
> 63         semops.push(user_space.read_val::<SemBuf>(tsops + size_of::<SemBuf>() * i)?);
> 64     }
> 65 
> 66     sem_op(sem_id, semops, timeout, ctx)?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `do_sys_semtimedop()` copies all user `SemBuf` entries and passes them inward without validating every `sem_num` at the syscall boundary. With `sops[0]` waiting on a valid nonzero semaphore and `sops[1].sem_num >= nsems`, `perform_atomic_semop()` returns `Ok(false)` before checking `sops[1]`, so the invalid user request is enqueued instead of returning `EFBIG` immediately.

**Fix.** Validate the full `semops` vector against the target `SemaphoreSet` before it can be queued or executed. Alternatively, construct a validated internal operation type at the syscall boundary and make `sem_op()` accept only that type.

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/syscall/semop.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> +    sem_op(sem_id, semops, timeout, ctx)?;
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (minor): This change makes `semop` and `semtimedop` process a vector of operations atomically, but the Linux Compatibility documentation is unchanged and does not record the supported arguments, flags, or atomic behavior.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage page and matching `.scml` file to document the new multi-operation semantics, supported flags, and relevant errors.

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The change moves semaphore state under a set-wide lock and introduces vectorized, queued `semop` execution. The refactor improves the structure needed for atomic operations, and the review found no hardware-specific issues.
+
+The highest-risk defects are in the new blocking path: timed waits never retain the timer strongly enough to fire, invalid operations can remain queued forever, repeated operations on one semaphore are validated against stale state, and user-controlled `SEM_UNDO` reaches a kernel panic. The new `SEMNCNT`/`SEMZCNT` reporting also returns incorrect values, including for invalid indices.
+

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The change moves semaphore state under a set-wide lock and introduces vectorized, queued `semop` execution. The refactor improves the structure needed for atomic operations, and the review found no hardware-specific issues.
+
+The highest-risk defects are in the new blocking path: timed waits never retain the timer strongly enough to fire, invalid operations can remain queued forever, repeated operations on one semaphore are validated against stale state, and user-controlled `SEM_UNDO` reaches a kernel panic. The new `SEMNCNT`/`SEMZCNT` reporting also returns incorrect values, including for invalid indices.
+
+Before merging, separate the storage refactor from the behavior change, validate the complete operation vector before queueing, evaluate operations against tentative semaphore values, keep timeout timers alive and cancel them on completion, and move wakeups outside the spinlock. Enforce semaphore permissions and replace unsupported `todo!()` paths with explicit syscall errors. Update the compatibility documentation for the changed syscall behavior.
 
 ## Maintainability
 
@@ -207,7 +211,7 @@
 
 Stuck pending operation (major): `wake_const_ops` treats every `Err` from `perform_atomic_semop` as if the operation merely still needs to wait. A request such as `[{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 0 }, { sem_num: 999, sem_op: 0 }]` can be queued because the first operation blocks; after semaphore `0` reaches zero, the later `EFBIG` is ignored and the request remains queued forever.
 
-**Fix.** Validate every semaphore index and other terminal errors before enqueueing a request, and remove or report queued operations that fail with a terminal error instead of silently treating all errors as waiting.
+**Fix.** Shared with the `validate-at-boundaries` finding for `semop`: validate every semaphore index and other terminal errors before enqueueing a request, and remove or report queued operations that fail with a terminal error instead of silently treating all errors as waiting.
 
 ### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 329
 
@@ -235,7 +239,7 @@
 
 Reachable panic (critical): `SEM_UNDO` is a valid user-supplied flag, but the new multi-operation path executes `todo!()` when it encounters it. A successful call such as `semop([{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 1, sem_flags: SEM_UNDO }])` can therefore panic the kernel.
 
-**Fix.** Implement `SEM_UNDO` bookkeeping or reject the flag with an error such as `ENOSYS` before the operation can reach this path; do not use `todo!()` for user-controlled input.
+**Fix.** Implement `SEM_UNDO` bookkeeping or reject the flag with an error such as `ENOSYS` during syscall-boundary validation before the operation can reach this path; do not use `todo!()` for user-controlled input. Shared with the Security `Reachable panic` finding.
 
 ### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 84
 
@@ -253,7 +257,7 @@
 
 Wrong waiter count (major): `pending_const_count` initializes `count` to `1`, so `SEM_GETZCNT` reports one waiter even when the queue is empty. It also increments once per matching `SemBuf`, so one pending atomic request with duplicate zero operations is counted multiple times instead of once.
 
-**Fix.** Initialize the count to `0` and count pending requests, not matching `SemBuf` entries; a request should contribute at most once for the queried semaphore.
+**Fix.** Initialize the count to `0`, count each pending request at most once, and include only operations that actually wait for the queried semaphore condition (`sem_op == 0` for `SEM_GETZCNT`, `sem_op < 0` for `SEM_GETNCNT`). Shared with the other `Wrong waiter count` finding.
 
 ### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 98
 
@@ -271,7 +275,7 @@
 
 Wrong waiter count (major): `pending_alter_count` has the same off-by-one and per-entry counting errors, so `SEM_GETNCNT` returns `1` with no pending operations and overcounts atomic requests containing multiple matching operations.
 
-**Fix.** Start from `0` and count each pending request once for the semaphore on which it is actually waiting.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `Wrong waiter count` finding: initialize the count to `0`, count each pending request at most once, and include only operations that actually wait for the queried semaphore condition (`sem_op == 0` for `SEM_GETZCNT`, `sem_op < 0` for `SEM_GETNCNT`).
 
 ### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83
 
@@ -285,7 +289,7 @@
 
 Invalid semaphore index accepted (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT` cast the user-supplied `semnum` to `u16` and call count helpers that never validate membership. `semnum = nsems` succeeds instead of returning `EINVAL`, and `semnum = 65536` wraps to semaphore `0`.
 
-**Fix.** Validate the original `semnum` as a `usize` against `sem_set.nsems()` before counting, and avoid the narrowing `u16` conversion.
+**Fix.** Shared with the Security `validate-at-boundaries` finding for `SEM_GETZCNT`/`SEM_GETNCNT`: validate the original `semnum` as a `usize` against `sem_set.nsems()` before counting, and avoid the narrowing `u16` conversion.
 
 ## Security
 
@@ -314,7 +318,7 @@
 
 Reachable panic (critical): A userspace `SemBuf` can set `sem_flags` to include `SEM_UNDO`; once the operation is otherwise satisfiable, `perform_atomic_semop()` reaches `todo!()`, which panics in kernel code. For example, `sem_op = 0`, `sem_num = 0`, and `SEM_UNDO` on a zero-valued semaphore reaches this path.
 
-**Fix.** Remove the userspace-reachable `todo!()`: either implement `SEM_UNDO` handling or reject unsupported `SEM_UNDO` requests during syscall-boundary validation before any operation can be applied or enqueued.
+**Fix.** Shared with the Correctness `Reachable panic` finding: remove the userspace-reachable `todo!()` by implementing `SEM_UNDO` handling or rejecting unsupported `SEM_UNDO` requests during syscall-boundary validation before any operation can be applied or enqueued.
 
 ### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83
 
@@ -331,7 +335,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT` cast the user-supplied `semnum` to `u16` instead of validating it against `nsems`. A hostile caller can pass `semnum = 65536` and have it silently treated as semaphore `0`, so an out-of-range syscall argument is accepted and used internally.
 
-**Fix.** Keep `semnum` as `usize` and validate it with the target `SemaphoreSet` before counting, returning `EINVAL` for `semnum >= nsems`; do the same for both `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the Correctness `Invalid semaphore index accepted` finding: keep `semnum` as `usize` and validate it with the target `SemaphoreSet` before counting, returning `EINVAL` for `semnum >= nsems`; do the same for both `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/syscall/semop.rs` line 63
 
@@ -347,7 +351,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (major): `do_sys_semtimedop()` copies all user `SemBuf` entries and passes them inward without validating every `sem_num` at the syscall boundary. With `sops[0]` waiting on a valid nonzero semaphore and `sops[1].sem_num >= nsems`, `perform_atomic_semop()` returns `Ok(false)` before checking `sops[1]`, so the invalid user request is enqueued instead of returning `EFBIG` immediately.
 
-**Fix.** Validate the full `semops` vector against the target `SemaphoreSet` before it can be queued or executed. Alternatively, construct a validated internal operation type at the syscall boundary and make `sem_op()` accept only that type.
+**Fix.** Validate the full `semops` vector against the target `SemaphoreSet` before it can be queued or executed. Shared with the `Stuck pending operation` finding: alternatively, construct a validated internal operation type at the syscall boundary and make `sem_op()` accept only that type.

# Summary

The change moves semaphore state under a set-wide lock and introduces vectorized, queued `semop` execution. The refactor improves the structure needed for atomic operations, and the review found no hardware-specific issues.

The highest-risk defects are in the new blocking path: timed waits never retain the timer strongly enough to fire, invalid operations can remain queued forever, repeated operations on one semaphore are validated against stale state, and user-controlled `SEM_UNDO` reaches a kernel panic. The new `SEMNCNT`/`SEMZCNT` reporting also returns incorrect values, including for invalid indices.

Before merging, separate the storage refactor from the behavior change, validate the complete operation vector before queueing, evaluate operations against tentative semaphore values, keep timeout timers alive and cancel them on completion, and move wakeups outside the spinlock. Enforce semaphore permissions and replace unsupported `todo!()` paths with explicit syscall errors. Update the compatibility documentation for the changed syscall behavior.

## Maintainability

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> ```

`imperative-subject` (nit): The subject is imperative and within the length limit, but the syscall identifier `semop` is not wrapped in backticks as required by the commit-message convention.

**Fix.** Keep the imperative subject and wrap the syscall identifier `semop` in backticks.

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> 
> val: SpinLock<i32> -> val: i32
> sem_buf: SemBuf -> sops: Vec<SemBuf>
> sem_op(&SemBuf) -> sem_op(Vec<SemBuf>)
> ```

`refactor-then-feature` (major): This commit combines the representation and locking refactor (`Semaphore` loses its per-value lock and `SemaphoreSet` gains `SemSetInner`) with the new multi-operation `semop` execution and wait queues. A failure in the new semantics cannot be isolated from the storage rewrite during review or bisection.

# Summary

The change moves semaphore state under a set-wide lock and introduces vectorized, queued `semop` execution. The refactor improves the structure needed for atomic operations, and the review found no hardware-specific issues.

The highest-risk defects are in the new blocking path: timed waits never retain the timer strongly enough to fire, invalid operations can remain queued forever, repeated operations on one semaphore are validated against stale state, and user-controlled `SEM_UNDO` reaches a kernel panic. The new `SEMNCNT`/`SEMZCNT` reporting also returns incorrect values, including for invalid indices.

Before merging, separate the storage refactor from the behavior change, validate the complete operation vector before queueing, evaluate operations against tentative semaphore values, keep timeout timers alive and cancel them on completion, and move wakeups outside the spinlock. Enforce semaphore permissions and replace unsupported `todo!()` paths with explicit syscall errors. Update the compatibility documentation for the changed syscall behavior.

## Maintainability

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> ```

`imperative-subject` (nit): The subject is imperative and within the length limit, but the syscall identifier `semop` is not wrapped in backticks as required by the commit-message convention.

**Fix.** Keep the imperative subject and wrap the syscall identifier `semop` in backticks.

### `commit 6ce50fab1 message`

> ```diff
> Refactor semaphore to support atomic semop
> 
> val: SpinLock<i32> -> val: i32
> sem_buf: SemBuf -> sops: Vec<SemBuf>
> sem_op({prompt}SemBuf) -> sem_op(Vec<SemBuf>)
> ```

`refactor-then-feature` (major): This commit combines the representation and locking refactor (`Semaphore` loses its per-value lock and `SemaphoreSet` gains `SemSetInner`) with the new multi-operation `semop` execution and wait queues. A failure in the new semantics cannot be isolated from the storage rewrite during review or bisection.

**Fix.** Split this into an earlier behavior-preserving refactor of `Semaphore` and `SemaphoreSet`, followed by a separate commit implementing atomic `semop` and its queueing and wakeup behavior.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 43

> ```diff
> pub enum Status {
>     Normal = 0,
>     Pending = 1,
>     Removed = 2,
> }
> 
> pub struct PendingOp {
>     sops: Vec<SemBuf>,
>     status: Arc<AtomicStatus>,
>     waker: Option<Arc<Waker>>,
>     pid: Pid,
> }
> ```

`narrow-visibility` (minor): The new `Status`, `PendingOp`, and all four `PendingOp` methods are `pub`, but their only current consumers are the sibling `sem.rs` and `sem_set.rs` modules. This exposes implementation-only wait state through the crate-wide interface.

**Fix.** Use `pub(super)` for `Status`, `PendingOp`, and the cross-module methods, leaving members private wherever a sibling does not need them.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 156

`bool-names` (minor): The new booleans `alter` and `dupsop` do not read as assertions, and `dupsop` is an unexplained abbreviation. At `if alter`, the reader must infer that it means the operation contains a nonzero `sem_op`.

**Fix.** Use names such as `has_altering_op` and `has_duplicate_sem_num`, and return a small named type if both flags remain part of the helper result.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 182

`single-responsibility` (minor): The changed `sem_op` handles operation classification and execution, waiter and timer construction, queue insertion, lock lifetime, timeout cleanup, and final status decoding in one function. The syscall-level flow is buried under synchronization mechanics.

**Fix.** Extract the blocking lifecycle into a helper such as `wait_for_pending_op` that owns waiter and timer setup, enqueueing, cancellation, and status-to-error conversion; leave `sem_op` to coordinate lookup and atomic execution.

**Fix.** Rewrite the summaries with third-person verbs, for example `Updates pending operations`, `Looks for completable alter operations`, `Wakes pending zero operations`, and `Performs an atomic semaphore operation`.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 59

`accurate-names` (minor): `pending_const`, `pending_const_count`, and `wake_const_ops` describe the queue as `const`, while its meaning is `sem_op == 0` and nearby code calls the same case `zero`. `const` is easily read as a compile-time constant rather than a zero-wait operation.

**Fix.** Rename the queue and APIs consistently to `pending_zero`, `pending_zero_count`, and `wake_zero_ops`, and update the related comments.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 143

> ```diff
> pub fn get<T>({prompt}self, sem_num: usize, func: {prompt}dyn Fn({prompt}Semaphore) -> T) -> Result<T> {
>     let inner = self.inner();
>     Ok(func(
>         inner.sems.get(sem_num).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?,
>     ))
> }
> ```

`information-hiding` (major): The new public `get` method exposes the internal `Semaphore` type and a storage-oriented callback to callers. `sys_semctl` must import `Semaphore` and define `sem_val` and `sem_pid` callbacks just to read two fields, so representation changes become cross-module API changes.

**Fix.** Add dedicated operations on `SemaphoreSet`, such as `val(sem_num)` and `latest_modified_pid(sem_num)`, and keep `Semaphore` and its field accessors private to the semaphore implementation.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 158

`narrow-visibility` (minor): The timestamp mutators `update_ctime` and `update_otime` are exposed as `pub`, even though current call sites are inside `SemaphoreSet` and `sem.rs`. Unrelated code can therefore mutate IPC metadata without going through the operation that owns the state.

**Fix.** Make `update_ctime` private and `update_otime` `pub(super)`, or keep timestamp updates behind the corresponding semaphore-set operations.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 154

> ```diff
> // TODO: Support permission check
> warn!("Semaphore operation doesn't support permission check now");
> ```

`log-levels` (minor): This `warn!` executes on every `semop`, including successful operations, so normal syscall traffic floods warning logs with an expected unsupported-feature message.

**Fix.** Use `debug!` for routine diagnostics, or emit a warning only when the call actually takes an exceptional permission-handling path.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 173

> ```diff
> for wake_op in wake_queue {
>     wake_op.set_status(Status::Normal);
>     if let Some(waker) = wake_op.waker {
>         waker.wake_up();
>     }
> }
> ```

`no-io-under-spinlock` (major): `waker.wake_up()` calls the scheduler's `unpark_target` while `inner` is still held as a `SpinLock`. This performs scheduler queue work in the spinlock critical section; the same pattern also appears in `SemaphoreSet::setval` and `SemaphoreSet::drop`.

**Fix.** Detach the wake list, release `inner` and any other spinlock guards, then set statuses and call `wake_up()` outside the critical section.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 190

> ```diff
> let jiffies_timer = JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER.get().unwrap().create_timer(move || {
>     waker.wake_up();
> });
> jiffies_timer.set_timeout(Timeout::After(timeout));
> ```

Timer lifetime (major): `jiffies_timer` is the only strong `Arc<Timer>` and is dropped when the `if let` block ends. The timer manager retains only a callback containing a `Weak<Timer>`, so the callback cannot upgrade the timer and never calls `waker.wake_up()`. A blocked `semtimedop` therefore does not time out.

**Fix.** Keep the timer `Arc` alive until the waiter finishes, and cancel it when the operation completes before the deadline.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 292

> ```diff
> while let Some(const_op) = cursor.current() {
>     if let Ok(true) = perform_atomic_semop(sems, const_op) {
>         wake_queue.append({prompt}mut cursor.remove_current_as_list().unwrap());
>     } else {
>         cursor.move_next();
>     }
> }
> ```

Stuck pending operation (major): `wake_const_ops` treats every `Err` from `perform_atomic_semop` as if the operation merely still needs to wait. A request such as `[{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 0 }, { sem_num: 999, sem_op: 0 }]` can be queued because the first operation blocks; after semaphore `0` reaches zero, the later `EFBIG` is ignored and the request remains queued forever.

**Fix.** Shared with the `validate-at-boundaries` finding for `semop`: validate every semaphore index and other terminal errors before enqueueing a request, and remove or report queued operations that fail with a terminal error instead of silently treating all errors as waiting.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 329

> ```diff
> for op in pending_op.sops_iter() {
>     let sem = sems.get(op.sem_num as usize).ok_or(Errno::EFBIG)?;
>     let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(op.sem_flags as u32);
>     result = sem.val();
> 
>     result += i32::from(op.sem_op);
> }
> ```

Incorrect cumulative state (major): `result = sem.val()` reloads the original value for every `sop`, so repeated operations on one semaphore are not evaluated sequentially. For example, with `sem0 = 0`, `[{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 1 }, { sem_num: 0, sem_op: -1 }]` incorrectly blocks, while multiple positive operations can commit a final value above `SEMVMX`.

**Fix.** Track tentative values or accumulated deltas per semaphore during validation, then commit the complete operation only after every `sop` succeeds.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 353

> ```diff
> if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
>     todo!()
> }
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): `SEM_UNDO` is a valid user-supplied flag, but the new multi-operation path executes `todo!()` when it encounters it. A successful call such as `semop([{ sem_num: 0, sem_op: 1, sem_flags: SEM_UNDO }])` can therefore panic the kernel.

**Fix.** Implement `SEM_UNDO` bookkeeping or reject the flag with an error such as `ENOSYS` during syscall-boundary validation before the operation can reach this path; do not use `todo!()` for user-controlled input. Shared with the Security `Reachable panic` finding.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 84

Wrong waiter count (major): `pending_const_count` initializes `count` to `1`, so `SEM_GETZCNT` reports one waiter even when the queue is empty. It also increments once per matching `SemBuf`, so one pending atomic request with duplicate zero operations is counted multiple times instead of once.

**Fix.** Initialize the count to `0`, count each pending request at most once, and include only operations that actually wait for the queried semaphore condition (`sem_op == 0` for `SEM_GETZCNT`, `sem_op < 0` for `SEM_GETNCNT`). Shared with the other `Wrong waiter count` finding.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem_set.rs` line 98

Wrong waiter count (major): `pending_alter_count` has the same off-by-one and per-entry counting errors, so `SEM_GETNCNT` returns `1` with no pending operations and overcounts atomic requests containing multiple matching operations.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `Wrong waiter count` finding: initialize the count to `0`, count each pending request at most once, and include only operations that actually wait for the queried semaphore condition (`sem_op == 0` for `SEM_GETZCNT`, `sem_op < 0` for `SEM_GETNCNT`).

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83

> ```diff
> IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETZCNT => {
>     let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
>         Ok(sem_set.pending_const_count(semnum as u16))
>     })?;
> }
> ```

Invalid semaphore index accepted (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT` cast the user-supplied `semnum` to `u16` and call count helpers that never validate membership. `semnum = nsems` succeeds instead of returning `EINVAL`, and `semnum = 65536` wraps to semaphore `0`.

**Fix.** Shared with the Security `validate-at-boundaries` finding for `SEM_GETZCNT`/`SEM_GETNCNT`: validate the original `semnum` as a `usize` against `sem_set.nsems()` before counting, and avoid the narrowing `u16` conversion.

## Security

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 153

> ```diff
> 153     // TODO: Support permission check
> 154     warn!("Semaphore operation doesn't support permission check now");
> ...
> 167     if perform_atomic_semop({prompt}mut inner.sems, {prompt}mut pending_op)? {
> ```

Permission bypass (critical): `sem_op()` only logs that permission checks are unsupported and then continues to apply or enqueue the operation. A caller that knows a valid `sem_id` can therefore perform `sem_op != 0` operations on a semaphore set without `ALTER` permission, modifying IPC state owned by another user.

**Fix.** Before calling `perform_atomic_semop()`, compute the required `PermissionMode` from `sops` and reject unauthorized callers. For example, require `PermissionMode::ALTER` for any `SemBuf` with `sem_op != 0` and `PermissionMode::READ` for wait-for-zero operations, then call the same permission machinery used by `semctl` instead of only warning.

### `kernel/src/ipc/semaphore/system_v/sem.rs` line 353

> ```diff
> 328         let flags = IpcFlags::from_bits_truncate(op.sem_flags as u32);
> ...
> 352         if flags.contains(IpcFlags::SEM_UNDO) {
> 353             todo!()
> 354         }
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): A userspace `SemBuf` can set `sem_flags` to include `SEM_UNDO`; once the operation is otherwise satisfiable, `perform_atomic_semop()` reaches `todo!()`, which panics in kernel code. For example, `sem_op = 0`, `sem_num = 0`, and `SEM_UNDO` on a zero-valued semaphore reaches this path.

**Fix.** Shared with the Correctness `Reachable panic` finding: remove the userspace-reachable `todo!()` by implementing `SEM_UNDO` handling or rejecting unsupported `SEM_UNDO` requests during syscall-boundary validation before any operation can be applied or enqueued.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semctl.rs` line 83

> ```diff
> 81         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETZCNT => {
> 82             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
> 83                 Ok(sem_set.pending_const_count(semnum as u16))
> 84             })?;
> ...
> 88         IpcControlCmd::SEM_GETNCNT => {
> 89             let cnt: usize = check_and_ctl(semid, PermissionMode::READ, |sem_set| {
> 90                 Ok(sem_set.pending_alter_count(semnum as u16))
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT` cast the user-supplied `semnum` to `u16` instead of validating it against `nsems`. A hostile caller can pass `semnum = 65536` and have it silently treated as semaphore `0`, so an out-of-range syscall argument is accepted and used internally.

**Fix.** Shared with the Correctness `Invalid semaphore index accepted` finding: keep `semnum` as `usize` and validate it with the target `SemaphoreSet` before counting, returning `EINVAL` for `semnum >= nsems`; do the same for both `SEM_GETZCNT` and `SEM_GETNCNT`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/semop.rs` line 63

> ```diff
> 60     let user_space = ctx.get_user_space();
> 61     let mut semops = Vec::with_capacity(nsops);
> 62     for i in 0..nsops {
> 63         semops.push(user_space.read_val::<SemBuf>(tsops + size_of::<SemBuf>() * i)?);
> 64     }
> 65 
> 66     sem_op(sem_id, semops, timeout, ctx)?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `do_sys_semtimedop()` copies all user `SemBuf` entries and passes them inward without validating every `sem_num` at the syscall boundary. With `sops[0]` waiting on a valid nonzero semaphore and `sops[1].sem_num >= nsems`, `perform_atomic_semop()` returns `Ok(false)` before checking `sops[1]`, so the invalid user request is enqueued instead of returning `EFBIG` immediately.

**Fix.** Validate the full `semops` vector against the target `SemaphoreSet` before it can be queued or executed. Shared with the `Stuck pending operation` finding: alternatively, construct a validated internal operation type at the syscall boundary and make `sem_op()` accept only that type.

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/syscall/semop.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> +    sem_op(sem_id, semops, timeout, ctx)?;
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (minor): This change makes `semop` and `semtimedop` process a vector of operations atomically, but the Linux Compatibility documentation is unchanged and does not record the supported arguments, flags, or atomic behavior.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage page and matching `.scml` file to document the new multi-operation semantics, supported flags, and relevant errors.
